A lot of things, actually. Recently, I just started lining up all these new commissions and messaging old customers out of nowhere. I even started building a plan for a new website and sketching out this whole new Appalachian-inspired collection all at the same time. It just felt like I had to get everything moving right then and there, you know? Like I couldn't just sit and work on one piece, I had to be pushing all these big projects forward at once.\n\nDoctor: Have you been making impulsive decisions, like spending large amounts of money or acting on ideas without thinking them through?\n\nPatient: No, I wouldn't say I've been doing anything like that. I mean, I'm busy with all these projects, but I haven't been blowing money or making crazy choices. I'm just taking on a lot of work at once. I haven't bought anything I didn't need or done anything impulsive like that.\n\nDoctor: Do you often find yourself talking a lot more than others in social situations?\n\nPatient: No, not really. I mean... I talk to my customers about their pieces, but I don't think I'm talking any more than usual. I haven't noticed myself taking over conversations or anything like that. Just the normal amount, I guess.\n\nDoctor: Are you finding that your difficulties with focus and taking on multiple projects at once are causing problems in your relationships or daily life?\n\nPatient: Yeah, it actually has been. I, uh... I missed a major custom delivery recently. My shop records just got so disorganized with everything going on that I actually had to stop taking new work for now. \n\nAnd my family has been checking on me a lot more lately, too. I guess they got worried about how far I’d slipped from my usual routines. So, yeah, it's definitely been causing some problems.\n\nDoctor: When did you first notice these difficulties with focus and taking on multiple projects at once?\n\nPatient: Well, it was right after I won an award at this big jewelry show. That's when things really sped up. I felt just... wired, I guess, and kind of out of control with all the projects. That whole stretch lasted for about two weeks.\n\nDoctor: Have these difficulties with focus and taking on multiple projects continued since the two-week period after the award?\n\nPatient: No, not really. After those two weeks, things kind of... settled down. I'm not feeling that wired anymore, and I'm not starting a bunch of new projects all at once like I was. I'm still trying to clean up the mess from it, but the actual feeling of being out of control and jumping from thing to thing... that stopped.\n\nDoctor: How long have you been experiencing difficulties with focus and organization in your daily life, including the period before and after the two-week stretch you mentioned?\n\nPatient: Well, the focus issues with all those projects were just during that two-week stretch. But... honestly, since then, I've just been feeling really down. This heavy, depressed, guilty feeling has been hanging over me for about a month now. It makes it hard to get organized or care about getting things back on track.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. And by the time I sit back down, I realize I've completely lost the whole work order. It's just getting really hard to get anything done.\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself fidgeting or feeling restless when you're sitting still?\n\nPatient: Well, it's not so much that I'm fidgeting... it's more like I just have to be doing something. It just felt like I had to get everything moving right then and there, you know? Like I couldn't just sit and work on one piece, I had to be pushing all these big projects forward at once.\n\nDoctor: Have you been making impulsive decisions, like spending large amounts of money or acting on ideas without thinking them through?\n\nPatient: No, I wouldn't say I've been doing anything like that. I mean, I'm busy with all these projects, but I haven't been blowing money or making crazy choices. I'm just taking on a lot of work at once. I haven't bought anything I didn't need or done anything impulsive like that.\n\nDoctor: Do you often find yourself talking a lot more than others in social situations?\n\nPatient: No, not really. I mean... I talk to my customers about their pieces, but I don't think I'm talking any more than usual. I haven't noticed myself taking over conversations or anything like that.
